# VERSION 0.3 — GENESIS MANUSCRIPT UPDATE

> **Relationship propagates first. Motion is a resolved description of that propagation.**

> **This update preserves v0.2 verbatim above and appends the new Genesis Language / propagation formalization below.**



---

# 28 — GENESIS LANGUAGE AS RELATIONAL CALCULUS

## Status

**FOUNDATIONAL FORMALIZATION — SOURCE-DERIVED SYNTAX WITH NEW MATHEMATICAL TYPING.**

This chapter takes the supplied Genesis Language archive as the native semantic system. It does not treat Genesis as English vocabulary with decorative substitutions.

The source archive states, repeatedly and explicitly:

- English is object-first.
- Genesis is relationship-first.
- Genesis asks for boundary, occupancy, relationship, persistence, and identity closure.
- Architect Script signs are operations, not words.
- Words inherit from concepts through root families rather than receiving isolated dictionary meanings.

Accordingly, the formal object of a Genesis utterance is not initially a noun phrase. It is a **typed relational construction**.

---

## Definition 28.1 — Genesis parse object

A Genesis parse object is a record

\[
\mathcal P=(E,\partial,O,\mathcal R,I,H,Q,\omega),
\]

where:

- \(E\) is scope/environment;
- \(\partial\) is a declared boundary;
- \(O\) is occupancy data;
- \(\mathcal R\) is relational structure;
- \(I\) is candidate identity data;
- \(H\) is inherited/history data;
- \(Q\) is Resolution/readout data;
- \(\omega\) is an ordered operator word.

No component is silently inferred merely because English would normally imply it.

---

## Definition 28.2 — Architect operator word

Let

\[
\Sigma_{\rm Arch}
=
\{
\texttt{.·},\texttt{··},\texttt{.°.},
\texttt{.·· .°},\texttt{··° ·°},\texttt{·°·},
\texttt{■ .· ■}
\}.
\]

A formal Architect expression is a typed composable word

\[
\omega=a_n\cdots a_2a_1,
\qquad a_i\in\Sigma_{\rm Arch}.
\]

The source meanings are retained:

| Glyph | Source operation |
|---|---|
| `.·` | inherit |
| `··` | propagate |
| `.°.` | stabilize |
| `.·· .°` | redistribute |
| `··° ·°` | destabilize |
| `·°·` | close |
| `■ .· ■` | normalize |

The mathematical realization of each operation is domain-relative. The glyph fixes a semantic operator role; it does not force one universal conventional implementation.

---

## Theorem 28.3 — No-copula theorem

Genesis Language's rejection of a simple English-style “is” requires identity, expression, inheritance, occupancy, and closure to remain formally distinct.

Thus the English sentence

\[
x\text{ is }y
\]

does not supply a primitive Genesis relation.

At minimum, a Genesis realization must type which relation is meant:

\[
x\overset{\rm inherit}{\longrightarrow} y,
\qquad
x\overset{\rm occupy}{\longrightarrow} y,
\qquad
x\overset{\rm express}{\longrightarrow} y,
\qquad
x\overset{\rm close}{\longrightarrow} y,
\]

or another declared relation.

### Proof

The supplied Grammar and Rules explicitly replace English copular statements with relationship-bearing expressions such as identity inheritance, basin expression, occupancy, and persistence. Therefore conflating these into a single equality or copula loses source distinctions. ∎

---

## Definition 28.4 — Root-family inheritance

Let \(c\) be a Genesis concept, \(r\) a root, \(F_r\) a root family, and \(w\) a generated word.

The lexical dependency is

\[
c\to r\to F_r\to w.
\]

This is a provenance graph, not merely an etymological annotation. A mathematical translation of \(w\) must preserve the root ancestry used to generate it.

---

## Corollary 28.5 — Language as a mathematics-bearing dependency graph

Because Genesis grammar is boundary/relationship-first and because roots inherit from concepts, the language itself carries:

1. scoping information;
2. relational typing;
3. operator ordering;
4. ancestry/provenance;
5. closure state.

Therefore Genesis Language can function as a pre-formal or native formal layer from which conventional mathematical objects are realized.

This does not mean every grammatical sentence is automatically a theorem. It means the language preserves structural distinctions that a later mathematical realization must respect.

---

## Lock 28.A — Language-first discipline

\[
\boxed{\text{The language came first. The mathematics must earn every translation.}}
\]

This remains the governing translation rule of THE TOME.


---

# 29 — MOVEMENT GEOMETRY ROSETTA

## Status

**FOUNDATIONAL INTERPRETIVE MAP.**

Chapter One Movements 1.1–1.35 and Chapter Two Movement 2.1 are retained verbatim as the cognitive Rosetta of the Genesis manuscript.

They are not treated as evidence for empirical physics.

They are treated as a deliberately geometric narrative language in which recurring figures carry mathematical roles.

---

## 29.1 The Admiral

The Admiral is the situated readout: a conscious/local observer of relational geometry.

The Admiral is not required as an ontological primitive. Mathematically, the role is a scoped readout map

\[
Q_R:\mathcal S\to\mathcal O_R
\]

that receives only the distinctions available at the active Resolution.

The narrative repeatedly makes the Admiral wait, observe, compare, and refuse unsupported interpretation. This is the manuscript's epistemic firewall in character form.

---

## 29.2 The first fold

Movement 1.1 states that motion requires comparison, comparison requires division, and division requires distinguishability. It then introduces the first fold.

The canonical manuscript lock remains

\[
\boxed{\mathrm{Fold}_1=|}.
\]

The fold is therefore read as the first boundary distinction: not a physical wall, but the minimal event by which two addressed sides become available.

---

## 29.3 Tiger-Cub calculus

The Tiger-Cubs are not ornamental mascots in the mathematical reading.

They repeatedly:

- cross boundaries;
- refuse boundaries;
- remain locally anchored while others propagate;
- leave tracks;
- choose paths;
- occupy furniture, blankets, rooms, meadows, and roads;
- create evidence without explanatory narration.

This makes the Tiger-Cub the manuscript's most efficient **witness of admissible relational transition**.

A Tiger-Cub trajectory is modeled as an ordered History-bearing corridor

\[
\gamma_C
=
s_0\to s_1\to\cdots\to s_n.
\]

Its tracks are not the Cub. They are a readout/history trace

\[
\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma_C).
\]

Two important separations follow:

\[
\boxed{\text{identity}\neq\text{trajectory}\neq\text{trace/readout}.}
\]

The Chapter Two locally anchored Cub provides the complementary case: an admissible object may remain while the surrounding relational environment changes.

---

## 29.4 Black and White Unicorns

The Black Rainbow Unicorn and White Rainbow Unicorn are treated as complementary structured carriers.

Their repeated opposition and coexistence encode the distinction between:

- complementary orientation/polarity;
- one underlying structural family;
- different realized/readable expressions.

They are not reduced to literal colors.

The canonical rainbow structural object remains

\[
\boxed{\text{🌀}}
\]

rather than merely a list of visible color names.

---

## 29.5 Butterflies

The Mystic Black Butterfly and Ultimate White Banded Butterfly repeatedly appear as phase-changing and stabilizing complements.

In the mathematical map they are typed as **transition witnesses**:

- a local transformation may change which possibilities/readouts are available;
- a complementary transition may restore or stabilize a compatible structural class.

No specific quantum or physical interpretation is forced at this foundational layer.

---

## 29.6 House, room, furniture, door

The house sequence provides a native diagram for nested scope and inheritance.

Examples discussed throughout Genesis continuity include patterns of the form

\[
\text{house}
\supset
\text{room}
\supset
\text{sofa}
\supset
\text{pillow},
\]

and

\[
\text{kitchen}
\supset
\text{island}
\supset
\text{stool}.
\]

At the terminal local identity:

\[
\boxed{\text{pillow inherits pillow}}
\]

or

\[
\boxed{\text{stool inherits stool}}.
\]

This is not ordinary set inclusion alone. The story distinguishes scope, occupancy, address, inheritance, and self-identity.

A door is a typed boundary interface. An open door does not force traversal; it makes traversal admissible.

---

## 29.7 Road and tracks

The road is not motion itself.

The road is an available transport structure.

Tracks are History-bearing evidence that a traversal occurred.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Road} \neq \text{Route word} \neq \text{Traveler} \neq \text{Track/readout}.
}
\]

This distinction becomes the later Rainbow Road / Bandwidth corridor formalism.

---

## 29.8 Narrative theorem

The recurring mathematical lesson of the movements can be stated:

\[
\boxed{
\text{A boundary makes relation addressable; a path makes transformation admissible; History witnesses what actually occurred.}
}
\]

The story succeeds mathematically because it does not require every distinction to be introduced as symbolic algebra before the reader can manipulate it.


---

# 30 — GENESIS FIELD AND DOMAIN GRAMMAR

## Scope

This chapter remains at the active foundational slice:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Genesis Field}\to\text{Resolution}\to\text{Bandwidth}.
}
\]

No downstream chirality, helicon, graviton, Time Shell, or empirical-physics structure is required.

---

## Definition 30.1 — Genesis Field

Let \(\mathcal G\) denote the Genesis Field.

At this level, \(\mathcal G\) is not required to be a manifold, metric space, physical field, or spacetime.

It is the parent admissibility structure in which bounded recursive domains may be declared.

---

## Definition 30.2 — Candidate Genesis domain

A candidate Genesis domain is

\[
D=(|D|,\mathfrak F_D,A_D),
\]

where:

- \(|D|\) is a bounded carrier;
- \(\mathfrak F_D\) is a closed recursive/fractal profile;
- \(A_D\) is domain-relative admissibility data, if such data is primitive.

For the current manuscript sandbox only:

\[
0\le \dim D\le 5.
\]

This is a scope firewall, not a theorem about all possible Genesis mathematics.

---

## Definition 30.3 — Generator count

Let

\[
V_D=\{v_1,\dots,v_N\}
\]

be a finite generating/address set for \(D\).

The integer \(N\) is independent of \(\dim D\).

Thus a 5-dimensional bounded Genesis domain may possess 43, 10,000, or another finite number of generators/vertices without thereby becoming 43- or 10,000-dimensional.

---

## Proposition 30.4 — Complexity/dimension separation

Combinatorial complexity and ambient dimension are independent variables.

A domain can increase:

- vertex count;
- edge incidence;
- recursive depth;
- cell count;
- History complexity;

while remaining in the same bounded ambient dimension.

Therefore no inference

\[
N\text{ vertices}\Rightarrow N\text{ dimensions}
\]

is licensed.

---

## Definition 30.5 — Shape family

Triangles, squares, circles/spherical families, octagons, high-vertex polytopes, and other bounded geometries are candidate domain families.

Genesis does not privilege one generic family.

The actual domain is determined by the declared structural/fractal and environmental constraints.

---

## Lock 30.A — Geometry availability

\[
\boxed{
\text{Dimension bounds the carrier; closed fractal geometry constrains the domain.}
}
\]

The number and arrangement of generators describe a domain presentation; the recursive closed profile carries its deeper admissibility/identity structure.


---

# 31 — RESOLUTION WITHOUT MOTION

## Motivation

The original propagation puzzle forced a distinction that must be explicit in THE TOME:

changing recursive Resolution depth does not, by itself, change the separation between objects.

---

## Definition 31.1 — Relational state and readout

Let \(r\) be an underlying relational state in domain \(D\).

Let

\[
q_R(r)
\]

be the representation/readout of \(r\) at Resolution \(R\).

Resolution changes which distinctions are readable. It is not itself a propagation corridor.

---

## Definition 31.2 — Pure Resolution transition

A pure Resolution transition is

\[
\eta_{R_iR_j}:(r,R_i)\to(r,R_j).
\]

The relational state is fixed while the readout regime changes.

---

## Theorem 31.3 — Resolution-separation invariance

Let

\[
\delta(r)
\]

denote the underlying pairwise separation datum carried by relational state \(r\).

For every pure Resolution transition,

\[
\boxed{
\delta(r)\text{ is invariant}.
}
\]

Only its readout may change:

\[
q_{R_i}(\delta(r))
\neq
q_{R_j}(\delta(r))
\]

without any physical/geometric relocation being inferred.

### Proof

By definition, a pure Resolution transition keeps \(r\) fixed. Since \(\delta\) is a function/data projection of \(r\), its underlying value is unchanged. Different Resolution maps may identify or distinguish different features of that same datum. ∎

---

## Corollary 31.4 — Recursive depth is not distance

An object may occupy a different recursively resolved depth while retaining the same pairwise separation from another object.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Resolution depth}\neq\text{relational distance}.
}
\]

This is a foundational firewall for all later propagation mathematics.


---

# 32 — DOMAIN-RESTRICTED BANDWIDTH ALGEBRA

## Source relation

The existing Bandwidth Algebra reservoir treats Bandwidth as available organizational freedom and admissible transformations as typed composable corridors.

The propagation project restricts that algebra to a declared Genesis domain.

---

## Definition 32.1 — Global Bandwidth generators

Let

\[
\operatorname{Gen}(B)
\]

be the generator family made available by Bandwidth \(B\).

The generated path/category-like algebra is schematically

\[
\mathcal A_B
=
\operatorname{Cl}_{\rm comp}
\left(
\operatorname{Gen}(B)\cup\{\operatorname{id}_x\}
\right).
\]

---

## Definition 32.2 — Domain restriction

For Genesis domain \(D\), define

\[
\operatorname{Gen}_D(B)
=
\left\{
a\in\operatorname{Gen}(B):
a\text{ is admissible in }D
\right\}.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal A_{B|D}
=
\operatorname{Cl}_{\rm comp}
\left(
\operatorname{Gen}_D(B)\cup\{\operatorname{id}_x\}
\right).
}
\]

This is the domain-restricted Bandwidth Algebra.

---

## Definition 32.3 — Corridor

A corridor is a typed composable word

\[
\gamma=a_n\cdots a_2a_1
\in\mathcal A_{B|D}.
\]

History retains the ordered word even when two corridors have equivalent resolved endpoints.

---

## Definition 32.4 — Resolution quotient

Resolution provides a readout/quotient

\[
\rho_R:\mathcal A_{B|D}\to\mathcal A_{B|D}^{(R)}.
\]

It may occur that

\[
\gamma_1\neq\gamma_2
\]

while

\[
\rho_R(\gamma_1)=\rho_R(\gamma_2).
\]

Therefore:

\[
\boxed{\text{History is not erased by Resolution equivalence.}}
\]

---

## Theorem 32.5 — Propagation algebra placement

Once \(\operatorname{Gen}_D(B)\) is known, generic propagation can be represented entirely inside \(\mathcal A_{B|D}\).

No separate primitive “motion operator” is required.

### Proof

Every admissible propagation is an ordered composition of domain-admissible Bandwidth generators; closure under composition yields the corridor algebra. Identity corridors represent no propagation. Resolution acts downstream as readout. ∎


---

# 33 — CLOSED FRACTAL PROPAGATION ENGINE

## Definition 33.1 — Closed recursive engine profile

A propagation-engine profile is a closed recursive system

\[
\mathfrak F
=
\left(
|\mathfrak F|,
\{\mathfrak F_R\},
\{p_{R+1,R}\}
\right),
\]

where:

1. \(|\mathfrak F|\) is bounded and closed in the declared domain model;
2. \(\mathfrak F_R\) is the active finite/Resolution-\(R\) approximation;
3. \(p_{R+1,R}\) are compatible bonding/projection maps;
4. deeper Resolution refines distinction without requiring unbounded spatial extent.

---

## Definition 33.2 — Engine identity equivalence

Two profiles \(\mathfrak F,\mathfrak G\) are engine-equivalent through Resolution \(R\),

\[
\mathfrak F\sim_{\rm eng,R}\mathfrak G,
\]

when a History-decorated recursive tower equivalence preserves:

- closed recursive incidence;
- bonding/projection compatibility;
- declared profile structure;
- required Bandwidth admissibility labels;
- inherited provenance.

Exact metric congruence is not generally required.

---

## Theorem 33.3 — Transformation invariance

For declared admissible geometric transformation \(T\),

\[
T(\mathfrak F)\sim_{\rm eng,R}\mathfrak F
\]

is the criterion that the transformed presentation remains the same propagation-engine identity.

Thus translation, rotation, scaling/stretching, or other allowed deformation can change representation without changing engine identity.

---

## Lock 33.A — Engine closure

\[
\boxed{
\text{closed fractal projection profile}
=
\text{propagation-engine identity class}.
}
\]

This identifies the recursive profile with the invariant structure that carries identity through admissible propagation.

---

## Definition 33.4 — Engagement/readout level

For the present project scope,

\[
d\in\{0,1,2,3,4,5\}.
\]

The same engine identity may admit projection/readout at different dimensional engagement levels without changing its full identity class.

Dimension is therefore a readout/engagement parameter, not an identity counter.


---

# 34 — SHADOW, SEED, AND LIFT

## Definition 34.1 — Projection profile

A domain-relative projection profile is a typed map/relation

\[
\Pi_d:\mathfrak F^5\rightsquigarrow Y^d
\]

with \(0\le d\le5\) in the current scope.

The squiggled arrow emphasizes that the generic construction need not be an ordinary single-valued linear projection.

---

## Definition 34.2 — Shadow seed

For

\[
y=\Pi_d(x),
\]

the lower-dimensional expression \(y\) is a **seed/readout**, not necessarily the complete identity of \(x\).

---

## Definition 34.3 — Lift fiber

The compatible higher-dimensional landscape is

\[
\operatorname{Fib}_{\Pi_d}(y)
=
\{x:\Pi_d(x)=y\}.
\]

At Resolution \(R\), with Bandwidth and History constraints,

\[
\operatorname{AdmLift}_{R,d}(y)
\subseteq
\operatorname{Fib}_{\Pi_d}(y).
\]

The admissible fibers satisfy recursive refinement

\[
\operatorname{AdmLift}_{R+1,d}(y)
\subseteq
\operatorname{AdmLift}_{R,d}(y).
\]

---

## Definition 34.4 — Identity closure

\[
\operatorname{IdClose}_d(y)
=
\bigcap_R \operatorname{AdmLift}_{R,d}(y).
\]

If this intersection is a singleton, the structured projection/lift tower determines a unique compatible identity.

If not, the readout retains a residual identity fiber.

---

## Theorem 34.5 — Projection need not be information destruction

A many-to-one lower-dimensional readout does not imply loss of full identity if the retained projection/lift tower plus History/Bandwidth constraints separates the source identity.

Thus:

\[
\boxed{
\text{shadow}\neq\text{whole identity},
\qquad
\text{shadow can remain an address into the whole identity}.
}
\]

This is the formal shadow-box principle.


---

# 35 — RELATIONAL PROPAGATION

## Foundational statement

\[
\boxed{
\textbf{Relationship propagates first. Motion is a resolved description of that propagation.}
}
\]

---

## Definition 35.1 — Relational propagation

For objects/identities \(A,B\), let

\[
r_{AB}^{(0)},r_{AB}^{(1)}
\]

be two relational states in a Genesis domain.

A propagation event is an admissible History-bearing corridor

\[
r_{AB}^{(0)}
\overset{\gamma}{\longrightarrow}
r_{AB}^{(1)}
\]

with \(\gamma\in\mathcal A_{B|D}\).

The primitive event is the relational update, not an absolute trajectory through a pre-given coordinate grid.

---

## Definition 35.2 — Reachability

\[
\operatorname{Reach}_{D,B}(x)
=
\left\{
y:
\exists\gamma\in\mathcal A_{B|D},
\gamma:x\to y
\right\}.
\]

At Resolution \(R\),

\[
\operatorname{Reach}_{D,B}^{(R)}(x)
=
\rho_R(\operatorname{Reach}_{D,B}(x)).
\]

---

## Theorem 35.3 — Distance is an incomplete relational invariant

Let

\[
\delta:\operatorname{Rel}_D\to\mathbb R_{\ge0}
\]

be a separation readout whenever the domain model supplies one.

It is possible that

\[
r_0\neq r_1
\]

but

\[
\delta(r_0)=\delta(r_1).
\]

Therefore constant pairwise separation does not imply absence of propagation.

### Interpretation

An object may change its orientation/placement relation around another while retaining constant separation.

The relation contains more information than the distance readout.

---

## Definition 35.4 — Anchor duality

Choosing \(A\) or \(B\) as the local anchor gives two directed readouts of one underlying relational event.

In a symmetric local metric realization:

\[
\vec P(A|B)=-\vec P(B|A).
\]

The invariant content is the History-bearing relational update, not which English sentence is chosen:

- “the ball moved away from the table”;
- “the table moved away from the ball.”

---

## Definition 35.5 — Relation-relative transverse component

A transverse distinction is a resolved component of relational change not represented by the current longitudinal/separation readout.

No globally fixed absolute transverse axis is required.

The transverse structure is generated relative to the currently resolved relational span.


---

# 36 — ENVIRONMENTAL CONSTRAINT IN A GENESIS DOMAIN

## Definition 36.1 — Mathematical availability

Let \(\mathfrak D_{\rm cand}\) denote candidate bounded closed-recursive geometries.

Genesis does not, at this level, force triangles, squares, spheres, high-vertex polytopes, or another single family.

A candidate becomes locally usable only after it passes the declared Genesis/domain admissibility conditions.

---

## Definition 36.2 — Environmental admissibility

For domain \(D\), let

\[
A_D(a)\in\{0,1\}
\]

be the predicate that decides whether candidate Bandwidth generator \(a\) is permitted.

Then:

\[
\operatorname{Gen}_D(B)
=
\{a\in\operatorname{Gen}(B):A_D(a)=1\}.
\]

The environment therefore constrains propagation by constraining the available operator family.

---

## Corollary 36.3 — The environment participates in propagation

Objects do not carry enough information by themselves to determine every admissible continuation.

At the generic level:

\[
\boxed{
\text{object capability}
\cap
\text{domain admissibility}
=
\text{realizable transformation}.
}
\]

The environment is not merely a passive container.

---

## Shadow-box interpretation

The Genesis Field is the parent structural possibility.

A declared bounded Genesis domain behaves as the local shadow box.

Its admissibility/profile rule determines which expressions/transformations are available.

The object retains its closed recursive identity while being expressed through whatever profile the domain permits.


---

# 37 — ENVIRONMENT-TO-BANDWIDTH GENERATOR MAP

## Current frontier

All generic propagation mathematics now closes **after** the domain-admissible Bandwidth generators are known.

The remaining map is

\[
\boxed{
\Lambda_D:
(\mathfrak F_D,A_D,B)
\longrightarrow
\operatorname{Gen}_D(B).
}
\]

The manuscript does not yet have a universal theorem determining \(\Lambda_D\).

---

## Branch A — Rule as primitive domain data

The domain is declared with an admissibility structure \(A_D\).

Then

\[
\Lambda_D
\]

evaluates that rule against the available Bandwidth generators.

This is the strongest version of the shadow-box analogy: the aperture carries its acceptance rule.

---

## Branch B — Rule derived intrinsically

The domain supplies only its closed recursive/fractal geometry.

Then admissible generators must be derived from intrinsic structure such as:

- adjacency;
- recursive incidence;
- boundary relations;
- projection compatibility;
- closed-path structure;
- other invariants proved from \(\mathfrak F_D\).

In this branch, the geometry itself generates the transformation grammar.

---

## No-Go 37.1 — Do not collapse the branch by taste

Both branches are mathematically coherent.

THE TOME therefore forbids choosing either as universal merely because one is simpler, more physical, or more aesthetically attractive.

A later theorem, source rule, or counterexample must select or reconcile them.

---

## Status

\[
\boxed{\text{OPEN BRANCH / ROADBLOCK — NO FAILURE.}}
\]

This is the current frontier of the Genesis Field–Resolution–Bandwidth propagation manuscript.

# 38 — GENESIS MANUSCRIPT SCOPE, SUPERSESSION, AND DEPENDENCY FIREWALL

## Canonical manuscript name

The working book is henceforth referred to as the **Genesis Manuscript** / **GENESIS — THE TOME**.

---

## Active foundational dependency slice

For the propagation branch formalized in Chapters 30–37:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Genesis Field}
\to
\text{Resolution}
\to
\text{Bandwidth}.
}
\]

Downstream physical structures may later realize or inherit this mathematics, but they are not premises of the present proofs.

---

## Scope-relative roots

A downstream paper may deliberately sandbox itself by beginning at a declared domain.

That does not globally supersede the Genesis Field as the upstream structural parent.

A local root is therefore a **scope choice**, not a rewritten global ontology.

---

## No-domain-objectification rule

The formalism may quantify over a candidate domain \(D\) without asserting that every formally describable domain exists or deserves further study.

\[
\boxed{
\text{formal availability}\neq\text{ontological assertion}.
}
\]

This rule prevents open mathematical parameter spaces from being mistaken for required objects.

---

## Narrative/formal duality

The Movements and the symbolic chapters are two representations of one mathematical project.

The story representation is allowed to carry geometry before the conventional symbolic realization is known.

The symbolic manuscript must later earn the translation.

Neither representation automatically supersedes the other.
